” are the Thom isomorphisms. Concretely, the projection formula allows us to identify the endofunctor
\mathrm{cof}(\epsilon_{a_{!}})\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{M}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{M}
with the endofunctor
-\otimes\mathrm{cof}(\epsilon_{a_{!}})(p^{*}(R))
; we may moreover identify
\mathrm{cof}(\epsilon_{a_{!}})(p^{*}(R))\simeq n_{\Delta}M\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}R
; and as we saw above, the
R
-orientation on
M
identifies the latter endofunctor with the
d
-fold suspension. The commutativity of the previous diagram allows us to identify the general Chas–Sullivan operation with the evaluation at
R
of the top-right natural transformation in the following diagram, after precomposition by
p(M^{X})^{*}\simeq e^{*}(p^{2})^{*}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{2}_{!}e_{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{2}_{!}\mathrm{cof}(\epsilon_{c_{!}})e_{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{!}\mathrm{cof}(\epsilon_{a_{!}})\Delta^{*}e_{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{!}\Delta^{*}e_{!}[d]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{!}(e_{1})_{!}\tilde{\Delta}^{*}[d]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{2}_{!}\Delta_{*}\Delta^{*}e_{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{2}_{*}e_{!}[2d]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{*}\Delta^{*}e_{!}[2d]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\Delta_{*}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TI}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces BC}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces PD}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\Delta_{*}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces PL}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces PD}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces PD}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

In the last diagram we use that
(U,\partial U)
is an
R
-Poincaré duality pair, obtained as the total space of a fibration pair
(U,\partial U)\to M
with base an
R
-Poincaré duality space, and fibre an
R
-Poincaré duality pair (in fact, a pair of the form
(*,S^{d-1})
); we have denoted by “
PL
” the Poincaré–Lefschetz duality isomorphism. The middle square commutes by
[
10
, Corollary 4.5.2]
, whereas the right square commutes by
[
10
, Theorem 6.4.3, Example 6.5.1]
.

We thus obtain that the general Chas–Sullivan operation is the evalutation at
R
of the following composite transformation of endofunctors of
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
:

\begin{split}&p(M^{X})_{!}p(M^{X})^{*}\simeq p^{2}_{!}e_{!}e^{*}(p^{2})^{*}\xrightarrow{\eta_{\Delta_{*}}}p^{2}_{!}\Delta_{*}\Delta^{*}e_{!}e^{*}(p^{2})^{*}\overset{PD}{\simeq}p^{2}_{*}\Delta_{*}\Delta^{*}e_{!}e^{*}(p^{2})^{*}[2d]\simeq\\
&p_{*}\Delta^{*}e_{!}e^{*}(p^{2})^{*}[2d]\overset{PD}{\simeq}p_{!}\Delta^{*}e_{!}e^{*}(p^{2})^{*}[d]\overset{BC}{\simeq}p_{!}(e_{1})_{!}\tilde{\Delta}^{*}e^{*}(p^{2})^{*}\simeq p(M^{Y})_{!}p(M^{Y})^{*}.\end{split}

6.3.
Recovering the operation (3)

Given a decomposition
Y\simeq X\sqcup_{\underline{2}}*\in\mathcal{S}
, we obtain the graph cobordism corresponding to the following diagram of spaces:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\emptyset}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\urcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}

The image of the previous along
Z_{3}
is the following diagram in
\mathrm{S}\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{R}
, in which the left square gives the 1-morphism
\mathfrak{e}_{\hat{\Psi}}
in

((\mathrm{S}\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{R})^{\text{l-lax}}_{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}/})^{2\simeq}
from Subsection
4.2
; we keep denoting
e=M^{\psi}
and
e_{1}=M^{\phi}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{M^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{R},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(1^{2}\,p^{2})_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(1^{2}\,\Delta)_{*}\bar{\Delta}_{!}p^{*}\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{!}p^{*}[d]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p(M^{Y})_{!}p(M^{Y})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[d]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p^{2})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\Delta_{*}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces BC}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p(M^{X})_{!}e^{*}}

corresponding to the composite transformation of endofunctors of
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
, whose evaluation at
R
gives the image of the above graph cobordism along
\mathrm{GFT}_{M}
:

\begin{split}p(M^{X})_{!}p(M^{X})^{*}&\simeq p(M^{X})_{!}e^{*}(1^{2}\,p^{2})_{!}\delta_{!}(p^{2})^{*}\xrightarrow{\eta_{\Delta_{*}}}p(M^{X})_{!}e^{*}(1^{2}\,p^{2})_{!}\delta_{!}\Delta_{*}\Delta^{*}(p^{2})^{*}\\
&\xrightarrow{BC}p(M^{X})_{!}e^{*}(1^{2}\,p^{2})_{!}(1^{2}\,\Delta)_{*}\bar{\Delta}_{!}\Delta^{*}(p^{2})^{*}\\
&\overset{2PD}{\simeq}p(M^{X})_{!}e^{*}(1^{2}\,p)_{!}\bar{\Delta}_{!}p^{*}[d]\simeq p(M^{X})_{!}e^{*}\Delta_{!}\Delta^{*}(p^{2})^{*}[d]\\
&\overset{BC}{\simeq}p(M^{X})_{!}\tilde{\Delta}_{!}(e_{1})^{*}\Delta^{*}(p^{2})^{*}[d]\simeq p(M^{Y})_{!}p(M^{Y})^{*}[d].\end{split}

In the previous composite we have labeled “
2PD
” the composite of two Poincaré duality equivalences.
We next observe that the composite

\delta_{!}\xrightarrow{\eta_{\Delta_{*}}}\delta_{!}\Delta_{*}\Delta^{*}\xrightarrow{BC}(1^{2}\,\Delta)_{*}\bar{\Delta}_{!}\Delta^{*}

agrees with the composite
\delta_{!}\xrightarrow{\eta_{(1^{2}\,\Delta)_{*}}}(1^{2}\,\Delta)_{*}(1^{2}\,\Delta)^{*}\delta_{!}\overset{BC}{\simeq}(1^{2}\,\Delta)_{*}\bar{\Delta}_{!}\Delta^{*}
; using this, we can simplify the previous transformation as the following one:

\begin{split}&p(M^{X})_{!}p(M^{X})^{*}\simeq(p^{2})_{!}e_{!}e^{*}(1^{2}p^{2})_{!}\delta_{!}(p^{2})^{*}\xrightarrow{\eta_{(1^{2}\Delta)_{*}}}\\
&(p^{2})_{!}e_{!}e^{*}(1^{2}p^{2})_{!}(1^{2}\Delta)_{*}(1^{2}\Delta)^{*}\delta_{!}(p^{2})^{*}\overset{2PD}{\simeq}(p^{2})_{!}e_{!}e^{*}(1^{2}p)_{!}(1^{2}\Delta)^{*}\delta_{!}(p^{2})^{*}[d]\\
&\overset{BC}{\simeq}(p^{2})_{!}e_{!}e^{*}(1^{2}p)_{!}\bar{\Delta}_{!}\Delta^{*}(p^{2})^{*}[d]\simeq(p^{2})_{!}e_{!}e^{*}\Delta_{!}\Delta^{*}(p^{2})^{*}[d]\\
&\overset{BC}{\simeq}(p^{2})_{!}e_{!}\tilde{\Delta}_{!}(e_{1})^{*}\Delta^{*}(p^{2})^{*}[d]\simeq p(M^{Y})_{!}p(M^{Y})^{*}[d].\end{split}

We next observe that the endofunctor
e_{!}e^{*}\colon\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{M^{2}}\to\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{M^{2}}
is equivalent by the projection formula to the endofunctor
-\otimes e_{!}p(M^{X})^{*}(R)
, and in particular it commutes with any
R
-linear natural transformation between
R
-linear functors. This allows us to rewrite the previous as the following composite:

\begin{split}&p(M^{X})_{!}p(M^{X})^{*}\simeq(p^{2})_{!}(1^{2}p^{2})_{!}\delta_{!}e_{!}e^{*}(p^{2})^{*}\xrightarrow{\eta_{(1^{2}\Delta)_{*}}}\\
&(p^{2})_{!}(1^{2}p^{2})_{!}(1^{2}\Delta)_{*}(1^{2}\Delta)^{*}\delta_{!}e_{!}e^{*}(p^{2})^{*}\overset{2PD}{\simeq}(p^{2})_{!}(1^{2}p)_{!}(1^{2}\Delta)^{*}\delta_{!}e_{!}e^{*}(p^{2})^{*}[d]\\
&\overset{BC}{\simeq}(p^{2})_{!}(1^{2}p)_{!}\bar{\Delta}_{!}\Delta^{*}e_{!}e^{*}(p^{2})^{*}[d]\simeq p_{!}\Delta^{*}e_{!}e^{*}(p^{2})^{*}[d]\\
&\overset{BC}{\simeq}p_{!}(e_{1})_{!}\tilde{\Delta}^{*}e^{*}(p^{2})^{*}[d]\simeq p(M^{Y})_{!}p(M^{Y})^{*}[d].\end{split}

Finally, we observe that the functor
(p^{2})^{*}
can be exchanged with all of the functors
(1^{2}p^{2})_{!}
,
(1^{2}\Delta)_{*}
etc., by symmetric monoidality of
\mathrm{S}\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{R}
.
We may therefore replace the previous with the following composite

\begin{split}&p(M^{X})_{!}p(M^{X})^{*}\simeq p^{2}_{!}(p^{2}1^{2})_{!}\delta_{!}e_{!}e^{*}(p^{2})^{*}\xrightarrow{\eta_{\Delta_{*}}}\\
&p^{2}_{!}\Delta_{*}\Delta^{*}(p^{2}1^{2})_{!}\delta_{!}e_{!}e^{*}(p^{2})^{*}\overset{2PD}{\simeq}p_{!}\Delta^{*}(p^{2}1^{2})_{!}\delta_{!}e_{!}e^{*}(p^{2})^{*}[d]\\
&\overset{BC}{\simeq}p_{!}(p^{2}1)_{!}\bar{\Delta}_{!}\Delta^{*}e_{!}e^{*}(p^{2})^{*}[d]\simeq p_{!}\Delta^{*}e_{!}e^{*}(p^{2})^{*}[d]\\
&\overset{BC}{\simeq}p_{!}(e_{1})_{!}\tilde{\Delta}^{*}e^{*}(p^{2})^{*}[d]\simeq p(M^{Y})_{!}p(M^{Y})^{*}[d].\end{split}

which agrees with the one given at the end of Subsection
6.2
by the equivalences
(1^{2}p^{2})\delta\simeq 1^{2}
and
p(p^{2}1)\Delta\simeq p
.

7.
Morphism spaces in
\mathrm{GrCob}

In this final section we analyse the morphism spaces in
\mathrm{GrCob}
, and prove in particular Theorem
B
. Throughout this section we abbreviate
\mathcal{C}=\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{S}}
.

7.1.
A colimit formula

Recall Definition
3.5
.
In this subsection we denote by
W\subset\mathcal{C}
the wide subcategory spanned by idle morphisms, so that we have
\mathrm{GrCob}:=L(\mathcal{C},W)
according to the notation from Subsection
2.3
.

As already observed in Remark
3.6
, any object in
\mathrm{GrCob}
is equivalent to the image of an object in
\mathcal{C}
of the form
(\emptyset\to X)
along the localisation functor.

Notation 7.1
.

We fix an object
x=(\emptyset\to X)\in\mathcal{C}
for the rest of the subsection; we let
W(x)
denote the full subcategory of
\mathcal{C}_{x/}
spanned by objects that are morphisms in
W
with source
x
, and we let
\pi(x)\colon W(x)\to\mathcal{C}_{x/}
denote the inclusion.

Notation 7.2
.

For
X\in\mathcal{S}
we abbreviate
\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}:=\mathrm{Fin}\times_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{S}_{/X}
.

Remark 7.3
.

Since
W
is defined as the class of morphisms in
\mathcal{C}
that are inverted along
D_{0}\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})
, we have that
W
satisfies the 2-of-3 property: given morphisms
f\colon x\to y
and
g\colon y\to z
in
\mathcal{C}
, we have that if any two of
f,g,gf
belong to
W
, so does the third.
As a consequence, we have an equivalence
W(x)\simeq W_{x/}
.

By Remark
3.6
, and in the light of Lemma
3.4
, we may identify
W
with the wide
\infty
-subcategory of
\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathrm{Fun}([1],\mathcal{S}))
spanned by morphisms that are sent to maps of finite sets along
D_{1}
, and to equivalences along
D_{0}
, i.e. with the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Fin}\times_{\mathcal{S}}\mathrm{Fun}([1],\mathcal{S})\times_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{S}^{\simeq}
. Similarly, we have an equivalence
W_{x/}\simeq\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
.

The goal of this subsection is to prove the following proposition.

Proposition 7.4
.

The data
\pi(x)\colon W(x)\to\mathcal{C}_{x/}
introduced in notation
7.1
give a left calculus of fractions at
x
for
(\mathcal{C},W)
in the sense of Definition
2.2
.

Definition 7.5
.

Given a graph cobordism between finite sets
B\to G\leftarrow A
, we say that a component of
G
is a
based tree
if it is contractible and its preimage in
A
consists of a unique point. Compare the terminology with
[
9
, Definition 3.5]
.

Definition 7.6
.

For
y=(B\to Y)\in\mathcal{C}
, we represent an object in
\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
by a diagram as in Notation
3.3
.
We say that an object is
reduced
if every based tree in
G
has preimage in
B
consisting of precisely one element.
We let
(\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X})_{\mathrm{red}}
denote the full subcategory of
\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
spanned by reduced objects.

Lemma 7.7
.

For an object
y=(B\to Y)\in\mathcal{C}
, the inclusion
(\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X})_{\mathrm{red}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
admits a right adjoint, which we denote

\mathrm{red}_{y}\colon\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}\to(\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X})_{\mathrm{red}}.

Proof.

We abbreviate
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
.
It suffices to construct for any object
z\in\mathcal{D}
a “candidate right adjoint object” in
\bar{z}\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{red}}
together with a morphism
\epsilon\colon\bar{z}\to z
, and check that postcomposition by
\epsilon
induces an equivalence of morphism spaces
\mathcal{D}(\tilde{z},\bar{z})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{D}(\tilde{z},z)
for all
\tilde{z}\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{red}}
.

We represent
z
as a diagram as in Notation
3.3
, and split
G=G_{1}\sqcup G_{2}
, where
G_{1}
consists of all based trees in
G
. We similarly split
B=B_{1}\sqcup B_{2}
and
A=A_{1}\sqcup A_{2}
, where
B_{1},A_{1}
are the preimages of
G_{1}
in
B,A
; note the equivalence
A_{1}\simeq G_{1}
. We may thus represent
z
by the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B=B_{1}\sqcup B_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\sqcup G_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\sqcup A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\urcorner}

We define
\bar{z}
and
\epsilon
using the following diagram, in which all maps are restricted from the above diagram for
z
, except for the left and the middle top horizontal maps, whose restriction to
B_{1}
is declared to be the identity of
B_{1}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B=B_{1}\sqcup B_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{1}\sqcup G_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{1}\sqcup A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\sqcup A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(B_{1}\to A_{1}\to X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sqcup(A_{2}\to X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\urcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(B_{1}\to A_{1})\sqcup\mathrm{Id}_{A_{2}}}

The left and middle square provide
\bar{z}
, and the decompositions of the top objects as disjoins unions are analogous to those above for
z
, and witness that
\bar{z}
is reduced.
The
\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
-coordinate of the morphism
\epsilon
is given by the map
(B_{1}\to A_{1})\sqcup\mathrm{Id}_{A_{2}}
of finite sets over
X
, i.e. by the dashed square above. If we consider this as a morphism in
\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathrm{Fun}([1],\mathcal{S}))
and compose it with
\bar{z}
, the resulting cospan of arrows of spaces admits an evident identification with
z
, upgrading the above dashed square to a morphism
\epsilon
in
\mathcal{D}
as desired.

We next fix
\tilde{z}\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{red}}
, and represent it as a diagram as in Notation
3.3
in which we decorate with a tilde the data which is specific of
\tilde{z}
, and we decompose
B
,
\tilde{G}
and
\tilde{A}
according to based trees in
\tilde{G}
just as above:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B=\tilde{B}_{1}\sqcup\tilde{B}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{G}=\tilde{B}_{1}\sqcup\tilde{G}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{A}=\tilde{B}_{1}\sqcup\tilde{A}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\urcorner}

The datum of a morphism
f\colon\tilde{z}\to z
in
\mathcal{D}
is then the same as a natural transformation of commutative diagrams of spaces as follows, in which the restricted natural transformation on top gives
\tilde{z}
, and the one on bottom gives
z
:

\hbox to243.4pt{\vbox to41.69pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 121.70084pt\lower-20.84723pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

We now observe that
\tilde{G}_{2}
is mapped to
G_{2}
along the map
f_{G}
. To see this, let
\tilde{\Gamma}
be a component of
\tilde{G}_{2}
, and let
\tilde{\gamma}
be its preimage in
\tilde{A}_{2}
; then either
\tilde{\gamma}=\emptyset
, or
\chi(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{\gamma})<0
, because
\tilde{\Gamma}
is not a based tree; the image of
\tilde{\Gamma}
in
G
will have the analogous property.

It follows that along the decompositions
B=\tilde{B}_{1}\sqcup\tilde{B}_{2}=B_{1}\sqcup B_{2}
we have a containment
\tilde{B}_{2}\subseteq B_{2}
and hence also
B_{1}\subseteq\tilde{B}_{1}
. From this, it follows that the above morphism
f
factors essentially uniquely as a vertical composite as follows:

Lemma 7.8
.

In the setting of Lemma
7.7
, the functor
\mathrm{red}_{y}
is a cocartesian fibration.

Proof.

We use again the abbreviation
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times\mathcal{C}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
. We first claim that the assignment
z\mapsto B_{1}
gives a well-defined functor
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{red}}\to\mathcal{P}(B)^{\mathrm{op}}
, where
\mathcal{P}(B)
denotes the poset of subsets of
B
; here the subset
B_{1}\subseteq B
is defined for
z
as in the proof of Lemma
7.7
, and it agrees with the set of based trees in
G
by the assumption that
z
is reduced. We need to check that if
f\colon z\to z^{\prime}
is a morphism in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{red}}
, then we have a containment
B^{\prime}_{1}\subseteq B_{1}
; this is proved by the same argument used in the proof of Lemma
7.7
for the containment
B_{1}\subseteq\tilde{B}_{1}
there.

We may now consider the functor
\mathcal{P}(B)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
, sending
S\mapsto\mathrm{Fin}_{S/}
; the cocartesian fibration associated with the composite
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{red}}\to\mathcal{P}(B)^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
is precisely the functor
\mathrm{red}_{y}\colon\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{red}}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
7.4
.

The first two requirements from Definition
2.2
are immediate, so we shall focus on the third.
Given an idle morphism
y^{\prime}\to y
in
\mathcal{C}
, we want to show that the induced functor
\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}\to\mathcal{C}_{y^{\prime}/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
induces an equivalence on classifying spaces.
Letting
y=(B\to Y)
and
y^{\prime}=(B^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime})
, the idle morphism induces an equivalence
Y^{\prime}\simeq Y
and can be regarded as a morphism
B^{\prime}\to B
in
\mathrm{Fin}_{/Y}
. Letting
y_{\emptyset}:=(\emptyset\to Y)
, we obtain a composition of idle morphisms
y_{\emptyset}\to y^{\prime}\to y
, so that it suffices to prove that the two functors with target
\mathcal{C}_{y_{\emptyset}/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
and with sources
\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
and
\mathcal{C}_{y^{\prime}/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
, respectively, induce equivalences on classifying spaces. We thus reduce to checking that for any
y=(B\to Y)\in\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{S}}
, the idle morphism
y_{\emptyset}\to y
induces an equivalence of spaces

|\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}|\xrightarrow{\simeq}|\mathcal{C}_{y_{\emptyset}/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}|.

This is immediate if
B=\emptyset
; an evident induction argument allows us to reduce the above to checking instead that any idle morphism
y^{\prime}\to y
induces an equivalence of spaces
|\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}|\xrightarrow{\simeq}|\mathcal{C}_{y^{\prime}/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}|
, under the following assumptions on
y
and
y^{\prime}
:
y
has the form
(B\to Y)
for a nonempty finite set
B
,
B^{\prime}=B\setminus\left\{b\right\}
,
y^{\prime}=(B^{\prime}\to Y)
, and the idle morphism
y^{\prime}\to y
is given by the inclusion
B^{\prime}\subset B
, considered as a map of spaces over
Y
.

Let
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\mathcal{C}_{y^{\prime}/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
.
By Lemma
7.7
the left and right arrow in the following composite functor induce equivalences on classifying spaces:

\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{red}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{idle}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{red}_{y^{\prime}}}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{red}}.

To prove that the middle functor induces an equivalence on classifying spaces, it thus suffices to check that the composite does. The middle functor. We observe that the functor
\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
, being a pullback of the left fibration of overcategories
\mathcal{C}_{y/}\to\mathcal{C}_{y^{\prime}/}
, is again a left fibration. By Lemma
7.8
the right functor is a cocartesian fibration, so that the composite functor
\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{red}}
is a cocartesian fibration as well. We claim that
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{red}}
is a subcocartesian fibration of
\mathcal{D}
. Since it is a full subcategory, it suffices to check that the target of a cocartesian morphism for
\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{red}}
starting at a reduced object in
\mathcal{D}
also ends in a reduced object. Let
z\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{red}}\subset\mathcal{D}
be an object as in Notation
3.3
, and let
z^{\prime}
be its image in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
along the above middle functor, which is essentially obtained by replacing
B
with
B^{\prime}
in the diagram for
z
; then there are two cases:

•

if the map
B\to G
sends
b
to a based tree, then
z^{\prime}
is not reduced, as
G^{\prime}=G
now contains a based tree whose preimage in
B^{\prime}
is empty; the object
\mathrm{red}_{y^{\prime}}(z^{\prime})
is obtained by discarding this based tree (together with its preimage in
A
), and it is therefore a reduced object;

•

if instead the map
B\to G
does not send
b
to a based tree, then
z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{red}}
and
\mathrm{red}_{y^{\prime}}(z^{\prime})\simeq z^{\prime}
.

To prove that the cocartesian fibration
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{red}}\to\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{red}}
induces an equivalence on classifying spaces, it suffices now to show that each fibre has contractible classifying space. Let therefore
z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{red}}
be represented by the following diagram, and let
\mathcal{D}^{z^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{red}}
denote the fibre at
z^{\prime}
of the above composite functor:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}_{1}\sqcup B^{\prime}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}_{1}\sqcup G^{\prime}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}_{1}\sqcup A^{\prime}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\urcorner}

We distinguish two types of objects
z\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{red}}^{z^{\prime}}
, represented by the following diagrams; in few words, in type (1) we have
b\in B_{1}
, whereas in type (2) we have
b\in B_{2}
:

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

In type (1) we lift
z^{\prime}
to
z
by adjoining a new based tree marked by
b
; in type (2) instead we use
b
to mark a component of
G^{\prime}
directly, and in order for the result to be reduced, we must send
b
to
G^{\prime}_{2}
in this case. The moduli space of objects of type (1) is therefore the space of lifts of the map
b\to W
to a map
b\to X
, i.e.
\mathrm{fib}_{b}(X\to W)
, whereas the moduli space of objects of type (2) is the space of lifts of the map
b\to W
to a map
b\to G^{\prime}_{2}
, i.e.
\mathrm{fib}_{b}(G^{\prime}_{2}\to W)
. All morphisms in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{red}}^{z^{\prime}}
are equivalences or go from type (1) to type (2). More precisely, a morphism between the objects (1) and (2) described above is the datum of:

•

a retraction of finite sets
B^{\prime}_{1}\sqcup\left\{b\right\}\sqcup A^{\prime}_{2}\twoheadrightarrow B^{\prime}_{1}\sqcup A^{\prime}_{2}
, which is the same as a point
a\in B^{\prime}_{1}\sqcup A^{\prime}_{2}
;

•

a homotopy between the map
\left\{b\right\}\to B^{\prime}_{1}\sqcup G^{\prime}_{2}
, which is a datum of the object (2) and factors through
G^{\prime}_{2}
, and the composite map
\left\{b\right\}\to B^{\prime}_{1}\sqcup A^{\prime}_{2}\to B^{\prime}_{1}\sqcup G^{\prime}_{2}
; in particular we must have
a\in A^{\prime}_{2}
, and we are choosing here a path in
G^{\prime}_{2}
from the image of
\left\{a\right\}\to G^{\prime}_{2}
to the image of the lift
\left\{b\right\}\to G^{\prime}_{2}
, which is a datum of the object (2);

•

a homotopy between the map
\left\{b\right\}\to X
, which is a datum of the object (1), and the composite map
\left\{b\right\}\to A^{\prime}_{2}\to X
; we are here choosing a path in
X
from the image of
\left\{a\right\}\to X
to the image of the lift
\left\{b\right\}\to X
.

The moduli space of morphisms of
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{red}}^{z^{\prime}}
going from type (1) to type (2), with arbitrary sources and targets, is therefore equivalent to the space of lifts of the map
b\to W
to a map
b\to A^{\prime}_{2}
, i.e.
\mathrm{fib}_{b}(A^{\prime}_{2}\to W)
. All in all the classifying space
|\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{red}}^{z^{\prime}}|
is equivalent to the pushout of spaces
\mathrm{fib}_{b}(X\to W)\sqcup_{\mathrm{fib}_{b}(A^{\prime}_{2}\to W)}\mathrm{fib}_{b}(G^{\prime}_{2}\to W)
, which is equivalent to
\mathrm{fib}_{b}(W\xrightarrow{\simeq}W)
, which is contractible as desired.
∎

The following is a direct consequence of Proposition
7.4
and Theorem
2.3
.

Corollary 7.9
.

For
X,Y\in\mathcal{S}
, the moduli space of graph cobordisms
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(Y,X)
is equivalent to the following classifying space of a left fibration over
\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
, or equivalently, to the following colimit over
\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
of a diagram of spaces:

\left|(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{S}})_{(\emptyset\to Y)/}\times_{\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{S}}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}\right|\simeq\mathrm{colim}_{(A\to X)\in\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}}\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{S}}((\emptyset\to Y),(A\to X)).

As consequences of Corollary
7.9
, we obtain the following further corollaries.

Corollary 7.10
.

The functor
D_{0}\colon\mathrm{GrCob}\to\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})
induces an equivalence on core groupoids. In particular
\mathrm{GrCob}^{\simeq}\simeq\mathcal{S}^{\simeq}
.

Proof.

Every object
X\in\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})
can be lift to an object in
\mathrm{GrCob}
along
D_{0}
, for instance
(\emptyset\to X)
: this shows that
D_{0}
is essentially surjective. Given
Y,X\in\mathcal{S}
, we consider the objects
x=(\emptyset\to X)
and
y=(\emptyset\to Y)
in
\mathcal{C}
. Our goal is to compute the subspace of
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(Y,X)
comprising morphisms
y\to x
that are invertible in
\mathrm{GrCob}
. The equivalence
\left|\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}W_{x/}\right|\simeq\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(Y,X)
sends an object
y\to x^{\prime}\leftarrow x
in the category
\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}W_{x/}
to the composite of the morphism
y\to x^{\prime}
and the formal inverse of the idle morphism
x\to x^{\prime}
; if we expand without loss of generality
x^{\prime}=(A\to X)
, this morphism in
\mathrm{GrCob}
is further sent along
D_{0}
to the morphism
D_{0}(y\to x^{\prime})
. A necessary condition for
y\to x^{\prime}\leftarrow x
being an invertible morphism in
\mathrm{GrCob}
is therefore that
y\to x^{\prime}
be idle in
\mathcal{C}
, and this is clearly also a sufficient condition. We thus obtain that the subspace of
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(Y,X)
comprising invertible morphisms in
\mathrm{GrCob}
is the classifying space of the
\infty
-subcategory
W_{y/}\times_{W}W_{x/}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}W_{x/}
. We may now identify
W_{y/}\simeq\mathrm{Fin}_{/Y}
,
W_{x/}\simeq\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
, and
W\simeq\mathrm{Fin}\times_{\mathcal{S}}\mathrm{Fun}([1],\mathcal{S})\times_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{S}^{\simeq}
as in Remark
7.3
; this leads to the identification of
W_{y/}\times_{W}W_{x/}
with the product of
\mathrm{Fin}_{/Y}
and the space
\mathcal{S}^{\simeq}(Y,X)
of equivalences
Y\xrightarrow{\simeq}X
; using that
|\mathrm{Fin}_{/Y}|\simeq*
, we obtain the desired equivalence
|W_{y/}\times_{W}W_{x/}|\simeq\mathcal{S}^{\simeq}(Y,X)
.
∎

Corollary 7.11
.

Consider
\mathcal{S}
as a wide
\infty
-subcategory of
\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})
, spanned by cospans of the form
Y\to W\overset{\simeq}{\leftarrow}X
; then
D_{0}
restricts to an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

D_{0}\colon D_{0}^{-1}(\mathcal{S})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{S}.

Proof.

By Corollary
7.10
we have that
D_{0}^{-1}(\mathcal{S})\to\mathcal{S}
is essentially surjective. To prove it is fully faithful, fix
Y,X\in\mathcal{S}
and let
y=(\emptyset\to Y)
and
x=(\emptyset\to X)
be the corresponding objects in
\mathcal{C}
. The preimage of
\mathcal{S}(Y,X)\subset\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})(Y,X)
along
D_{0}
is the subspace of
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(Y,X)
obtained as classifying space of the full
\infty
-subcategory
(\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}W_{x/})^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}W_{x/}
spanned by objects
y\to x^{\prime}\leftarrow x
such that
D_{0}(y\to x^{\prime})
is a morphism in
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})
; assuming without loss of generality that
x^{\prime}
takes the form
(A\to X)
, the requirement is that the morphism
y\to x^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{C}
be of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\emptyset}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\urcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

By Lemma
7.7
we have an equivalence
|(\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}W_{x/})^{\prime}|\simeq|(\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}W_{x/})^{\prime}_{\mathrm{red}}|
, where the index “
\mathrm{red}
” takes the full
\infty
-subcategory spanned by reduced objects. We now observe that all components of
G
in an object as above are based trees, so the condition of being reduced forces
G=A=\emptyset
. It follows that
(\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}W_{x/})^{\prime}_{\mathrm{red}}
is already a space, and as such it is equivalent to
\mathcal{S}(Y,X)
as desired.
∎

Corollary 7.12
.

The full
\infty
-subcategory of
\mathrm{GrCob}
spanned by finite sets is equivalent to
\mathrm{Gr}
.

Proof.

Denoting
\mathrm{GrCob}_{\mathrm{Fin}}
such full
\infty
-subcategory, the functor
D_{0}\colon\mathrm{GrCob}\to\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})
restricts to a functor
\mathrm{GrCob}_{\mathrm{Fin}}\to\mathrm{Gr}\subset\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})
, so it suffices to analyse morphism spaces in
\mathrm{GrCob}_{\mathrm{Fin}}
. For
X\in\mathrm{Fin}
, the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
has a terminal object
\mathrm{Id}_{X}\colon X\to X
, whence for
Y\in\mathrm{Fin}
the colimit from Corollary
7.9
is equivalent to the space
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{S}}((\emptyset\to Y),(X=X))
, and the latter can be readily identified with
\mathrm{Gr}(Y,X)
.
∎

Remark 7.13
.

Another immediate consequence of Corollary
7.9
is that for all spaces
Y,X\in\mathcal{S}
we have a composite pullback of spaces as in the following diagram, in which the left square is induced by the functor
D_{0}
and by precomposition with the graph cobordism
\emptyset\to Y=Y
, whereas the right square is given by forgetting the structure maps from
X
and from
Y
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(Y,X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{S}_{Y\sqcup X/})^{\simeq}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{S}_{Y/})^{\simeq}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(\emptyset,X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{S}_{X/})^{\simeq}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}^{\simeq}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{0}}

In particular, if the bottom map
D_{0}
is a subspace inclusion, then so is the top map
D_{0}
. This will allow us to reduce to the case
Y=\emptyset
in the proof of Proposition
7.24
.

Remark 7.14
.

The colimit formula for
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(Y,X)
in Corollary
7.9
is partially capturing the essence of Definition
1.1
: instead of directly attaching a graph to
X
, we attach it to a finite set mapping to
X
, but we now let this finite set vary in
\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
, which is a filtered category; so we may expect that this choice is actually inessential. We are on the other hand prescribing no specific relative cell structure on the pair
(G,A)
, but just the existence of such a cell structure (which boils down to the property of
G
of being equivalent to a finite graph). Using
[
9
, Theorem 8.4]
, if we understand relative cell structures on
(G,A)
as a datum to be given up to tree collapse maps, then this choice should also be inessential.

We will make the above considerations more precise in the next subsection.

We next present an example showing that
the functor
D_{0}\colon\mathrm{GrCob}\to\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})
does not induce an inclusion at the level of all morphism spaces.

Example 7.15
.

Let
Y=\emptyset
and let
X\in\mathcal{S}
be a simply connected space. The space
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(Y,X)
contains a subspace
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(Y,X)_{1}
comprising graph cobordisms as in Notation
3.3
such that
W\simeq X\vee S^{1}
. We abbreviate
\mathcal{C}=\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{S}}
and let
y=(B\to Y)=(\emptyset\to\emptyset)\in\mathcal{C}
. By Lemma
7.7
and Proposition
7.4
we may compute
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(Y,X)_{1}
as the classifying space of the full
\infty
-subcategory
\mathcal{D}
of
(\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X})_{\mathrm{red}}
spanned by reduced objects as in Notation
3.3
such that
W\simeq X\vee S^{1}
. There are two types of objects in
\mathcal{D}
, according to the size of
A
:

(1)

A\simeq\underline{2}
and
G\simeq*
; the moduli space of such objects is equivalent to
X^{2}/C_{2}
;

(2)

A\simeq*
and
G\simeq S^{1}
; the moduli space of such objects is equivalent to
X\times\mathbf{B}C_{2}
, i.e. the quotient
X/C_{2}
for the trivial
C_{2}
-action.

All morphisms in
\mathcal{D}
are either equivalences or go from objects of type (1) to objects of type (2); the moduli space of morphisms of the latter type is also equivalent to
X\times\mathbf{B}C_{2}
, with source map given by the diagonal, and target given by the identity; we obtain an equivalence
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(Y,X)_{1}\simeq X^{2}/C_{2}
. Passing to loop spaces, we obtain in particular that
\Omega\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(Y,X)_{1}
is equivalent to
\coprod_{2}(\Omega X)^{2}
.

We next consider the analogous subspace
\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})(Y,X)_{1}\subset\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})(Y,X)
spanned by cospans
Y\to W\leftarrow X
such that
W\simeq X\vee S^{1}
; this is a connected subspace, and its loop space is equivalent to
\mathrm{hAut}(X\vee S^{1},X)
, the space of homotopy automorphisms of
X\vee S^{1}
restricting to the identity on
X
. The space of self maps of
X\vee S^{1}
fixing
X
is equivalent to
\Omega(X\vee S^{1})
, and its subspace of homotopy automorphisms corresponds to the union
\Omega_{\pm 1}(X\vee S^{1})
of two connected components of
\Omega(X\vee S^{1})
, yielding the two possible generators of
\pi_{1}(X\vee S^{1})\cong\mathbb{Z}
.

The map of spaces
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(Y,X)\to\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})(Y,X)
induced by
D_{0}
restricts to a map of connected subspaces
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(Y,X)_{1}\to\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})(Y,X)_{1}
. Passing to loop spaces we obtain a map
f\colon\coprod_{2}(\Omega X)^{2}\to\Omega_{\pm 1}(X\vee S^{1})
; we have that
f
is a map of
E_{1}
-algebras for suitable
E_{1}
-algebra structures on the source and target; since both spaces consist of two components and since the map is a bijection on
\pi_{0}
, it suffices to study whether
f
is an equivalence on one component; we henceforth study the restricted map
f\colon(\Omega X)^{2}\to\Omega_{1}(X\vee S^{1})
.

Let us now specify
X=S^{k+1}
for some
k\geq 1
; then
\Omega_{1}(S^{k+1}\vee S^{1})\simeq\Omega(\bigvee_{\mathbb{Z}}S^{k+1})
is the underlying space of the free
E_{1}
-algebra in spaces generated by the pointed space
\bigvee_{\mathbb{Z}}S^{k}
, with basepoint serving as unit; similarly,
\Omega S^{k+1}
is the underlying space of the free
E_{1}
-algebra generated by the pointed sphere
S^{k}
. We stress that
f\colon(\Omega S^{k+1})^{2}\to\Omega(\bigvee_{\mathbb{Z}}S^{k+1})
is not a map of
E_{1}
-algebras when considering these free
E_{1}
-algebra structures and products thereof; instead,
f
agrees under suitable identifications with the following composite map of spaces:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{1}[S^{k}]\times E_{1}[S^{k}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{1}[\bigvee_{\mathbb{Z}}S^{k}]\times E_{1}[\bigvee_{\mathbb{Z}}S^{k}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{1}[\bigvee_{\mathbb{Z}}S^{k}],}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{0}\times i_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)\cdot(-)^{-1}}

where
i_{0},i_{1}\colon S^{k}\to\bigvee_{\mathbb{Z}}S^{k}
are two consecutive inclusions, and
(-)\cdot(-)^{-1}
denotes multiplication of the first element with the inverse of the second in the group-like
E_{1}
-algebra
E_{1}[\bigvee_{\mathbb{Z}}S^{k}]
. We then observe that
f
factors through
E_{1}[S^{k}\vee S^{k}]
, whose homology is much smaller than
E_{1}[\bigvee_{\mathbb{Z}}S^{k}]
: to wit,
H_{k}(E_{1}[S^{k}\vee S^{k}])\cong\mathbb{Z}^{2}
whereas
H_{k}(E_{1}[\bigvee_{\mathbb{Z}}S^{k}])\cong\bigoplus_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}
. We conclude that the map of spaces
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(Y,X)\to\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})(Y,X)
induced by
D_{0}
is not an equivalence for
Y=\emptyset
and
X=S^{k+1}
.

We invite the interested reader to generalise the above argument to the case of an arbitrary space
X
which is not aspherical.

Example
7.15
also shows that the conjecture from
[
39
]
is wrong in general: for a space
X
which is not aspherical, the canonical map from the classifying space of the category
G_{1}(X)
from loc.cit. to
\mathbf{B}\mathrm{hAut}(X\vee S^{1},X)
is not an equivalence. The identification
|G_{1}(X)|\simeq\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(\emptyset,X)_{1}
will be discussed in the next subsection.

7.2.
A formula via tree collapse maps

In this subsection, for
X\in\mathcal{S}
, we give an alternative formula for the space
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(\emptyset,X)
, in terms of the classifying space of an
\infty
-category
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)
of combinatorial graphs attached to
X
, with morphisms given by tree collapse maps. If
X
is an actual topological space, the
\infty
-category
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)
has been constructed as a category internal to topological spaces in
[
39
]
under the name “
G(X)
”. The main application of this alternative formula will be Proposition
7.24
, which will identify the space
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(\emptyset,X)
, building on work of Guirardel–Levitt
[
38
]
.

The main input towards the definition of
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)
is a variation of the (plain) category
\mathrm{Gaf}
of “marked graphs attached to a finite set” introduced in
[
9
, Definition 3.6]
: we simplify the description by removing the finite set “
B
” giving the marking, and by extending the setting to an equivariant one: this extra generality will be needed later in the proof of Proposition
7.24
. We fix therefore a discrete group
\Gamma
in the following; when
\Gamma
is the trivial group we recover the theory from loc.cit., specialised to the case in which the finite set “
B
” giving the marking is empty.

Notation 7.16
.

We denote by
\Gamma\mathrm{Fin}
the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbf{B}\Gamma,\mathrm{Set})
spanned by sets admitting a
\Gamma
-action with finitely many orbits; we refer to an object in
\Gamma\mathrm{Fin}_{\mathrm{free}}
as a
finite
\Gamma
-set
, even though the underlying set need not be finite. We further denote by
\Gamma\mathrm{Fin}_{\mathrm{free}}
the full subcategory of
\Gamma\mathrm{Fin}
spanned by sets with a free
\Gamma
-action, which we refer to as
finite free
\Gamma
-sets
.
We further abbreviate
\Gamma\mathcal{S}:=\mathrm{Fun}(\mathbf{B}\Gamma,\mathcal{S})
, and regard
\Gamma\mathrm{Fin}_{\mathrm{free}}\subset\Gamma\mathrm{Fin}
as nested full
\infty
-subcategories of
\Gamma\mathcal{S}
; we refer to an object in
\Gamma\mathcal{S}
as a
\Gamma
-space
.

Definition 7.17
.

A
\Gamma
-graph attached to a finite
\Gamma
-set
, shortly
\Gamma
-gaf, is a sequence
G=(A,V,H,\sigma,\upsilon)
consisting of a finite
\Gamma
-set
A
, two finite free
\Gamma
-sets
V,H
, and two
\Gamma
-equivariant maps
\sigma,\upsilon\colon A\sqcup V\sqcup H\to A\sqcup V\sqcup H
satisfying the following requirements:

•

\sigma
restricts to a map
H\to A\sqcup V
;

•

\upsilon
restricts to an involution of
H
without fixpoints, and the residual action of
\Gamma
on the quotient
E:=H/\upsilon
is again free;

•

with the above exceptions,
\sigma
and
\upsilon
restrict to the identity on each of the subsets
A,V,H
.

We say that
G
is a
\Gamma
-gaf attached to
A
. A
\Gamma
-subgaf of
G
is obtained by selecting
\Gamma
-subsets
A^{\prime},V^{\prime},H^{\prime}
of
A,V,H
, respectively, such that
\sigma
and
\upsilon
restrict to self maps of
A^{\prime}\sqcup V^{\prime}\sqcup H^{\prime}
.
A
\Gamma
-gaf
G
as above is a
based (respectively, unbased)
\Gamma
-tree
if
A
consists of a single
\Gamma
-orbit (respectively,
A=\emptyset
) and if the pushout of
\Gamma
-spaces
E\sqcup_{H}(A\sqcup V)
is
\Gamma
-equivariantly homotopy equivalent to the set
A
along the inclusion (respectively, to the set
\Gamma
).

A morphisms of
\Gamma
-gafs
G=(A,V,H,\sigma,\upsilon)\to G^{\prime}=(A^{\prime},V^{\prime},H^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime},\upsilon^{\prime})
is a
\Gamma
-equivariant map
f\colon A\sqcup V\sqcup H\to A^{\prime}\sqcup V^{\prime}\sqcup H^{\prime}
satisfying the following requirements:

•

f
restricts to a map
A\to A^{\prime}
;

•

f\sigma=\sigma f
and
f\upsilon=\upsilon f
;

•

for
v^{\prime}\in V^{\prime}
,
f^{-1}(\Gamma v^{\prime})
is a non-based
\Gamma
-subtree of
G
;

•

for
a^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}
,
f^{-1}(\Gamma a^{\prime})
is a disjoint union of based
\Gamma
-subtrees of
G
, one for each
\Gamma
-orbit in
f^{-1}(\Gamma a^{\prime})\cap A
;

•

for
h^{\prime}\in H^{\prime}
,
f^{-1}(h^{\prime})
is a singleton contained in
H
.

We denote by
\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma}_{\emptyset}
the category of
\Gamma
-gafs an morphisms of
\Gamma
-gafs. The index “
\emptyset
” only serves to warn the reader that we are working without markings, differently as in
[
9
]
. We further denote by
\mathfrak{A}^{\Gamma}
and
\mathfrak{AVH}^{\Gamma}
the functors
\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma}_{\emptyset}\to\Gamma\mathrm{Fin}
sending
G\mapsto A
and
G\mapsto A\sqcup V\sqcup H
, respectively, and by
\alpha^{\Gamma}\colon\mathfrak{A}\to\mathfrak{AVH}
the natural transformation evaluating at
G
to the inclusion
A\hookrightarrow A\sqcup V\sqcup H
. We let
\mathrm{Gaf}_{\emptyset}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{free}}
denote the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Gaf}_{\emptyset}^{\Gamma}
given by the preimage
(\mathfrak{A}^{\Gamma})^{-1}(\Gamma\mathrm{Fin}_{\mathrm{free}})
, and denote by
\alpha^{\Gamma}\colon\mathfrak{A}\to\mathfrak{AVH}
the restricted natural transformation of functors
\mathrm{Gaf}_{\emptyset}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{free}}\to\Gamma\mathrm{Fin}_{\mathrm{free}}
.
When
\Gamma=1
is the trivial group, we omit it from the notation, as well as the decoration “
\mathrm{free}
”.

We will mostly consider the subcategory
\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{free}}_{\emptyset}
.
Intuitively, an object in
\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{free}}_{\emptyset}
is a finite graph
G
with a free
\Gamma
-action and a partition of its set of vertices as
A\sqcup V
, where we consider
A
as the “attaching vertices” and
V
as the “inner vertices”; the set
H
of “half-edges” is endowed with an involution without fixpoints (the restriction of
\upsilon
), whose orbits form the set
E
of the “edges” of
G
; the restriction of
\sigma
gives the information about how to attach the half-edges to
A\sqcup V
. A morphism in
\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{free}}_{\emptyset}
from
G
to
G^{\prime}
is intuitively the datum of a map of finite free
\Gamma
-sets
A\to A^{\prime}
and a
\Gamma
-equivariant map of graphs
G\to G^{\prime}
exhibiting
G^{\prime}
as the result of collapsing disjoint trees in
G
(each intersecting
A
in at most one point) to distinct vertices, and of “base-changing” the attaching locus of the obtained graph from
A
to
A^{\prime}
, along a
\Gamma
-equivariant map
A\to A^{\prime}
.

Remark 7.18
.

For a space
X
we may consider the fibre product
\mathrm{Gaf}_{\emptyset}\times_{\mathrm{Fin}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
, using
\mathfrak{AVH}
on the first leg. By the equivalence of
\infty
-categories
\mathrm{Fin}_{/\mathbf{B}\Gamma}\simeq\Gamma\mathrm{Fin}_{\mathrm{free}}
, we then immediately obtain the identification
\mathrm{Gaf}_{\emptyset}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{free}}\simeq\mathrm{Gaf}_{\emptyset}\times_{\mathrm{Fin}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/\mathbf{B}\Gamma}
. The functor
\mathfrak{AVH}^{\Gamma}
corresponds to the projection on the second coordinate, whereas the natural transformation
\alpha^{\Gamma}
is recovered by concatenating the natural transformation
\alpha
and projection on the second coordinate.

Definition 7.19
.

We denote by
\Re^{\Gamma}\colon\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma}_{\emptyset}\to\Gamma\mathcal{S}
the functor sending a
\Gamma
-gaf
G
as in Definition
7.17
to the pushout of the following span diagram of
\Gamma
-spaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\sqcup V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\sqcup V\sqcup H}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\sqcup V\sqcup E.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)/\upsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}

We denote by
\beta^{\Gamma}\colon\mathfrak{AVH}^{\Gamma}\Rightarrow\Re^{\Gamma}
the resulting transformation of functors
\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma}_{\emptyset}\to\Gamma\mathcal{S}
. We use the same notation for the restricted functors on
\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{free}}_{\emptyset}
.

Concretely, we may think of
\Re^{\Gamma}(G)
as a “geometric realisation” of the combinatorial datum of a
\Gamma
-gaf
G
; we then have a
\Gamma
-equivariant map
\beta^{\Gamma}\alpha^{\Gamma}(G)\colon A\to\Re(G)
, corresponding to the inclusion of the attaching vertices.

Remark 7.20
.

When
\Gamma=1
, the functor
\mathfrak{A}\colon\mathrm{Gaf}_{\emptyset}\to\mathrm{Fin}
is a cocartesian fibration, whose straightening is the functor
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(\emptyset,-)\colon\mathrm{Fin}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
sending a finite set
A
to the (plain) category
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(\emptyset,A)
, which is the morphism category in the 2-category
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}
from
[
9
, Subsection 3.2]
. By Remark
7.18
, for general
\Gamma
we have that
\mathfrak{A}^{\Gamma}\colon\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{free}}_{\emptyset}\to\Gamma\mathrm{Fin}_{\mathrm{free}}
, being a pullback of
\mathfrak{A}
, is also a cocartesian fibration, but we will not need this generalisation in the following discussion.

Definition 7.21
.

For
X\in\Gamma\mathcal{S}
we define the
\infty
-category
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{\Gamma}(X)
as the following pullback in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
, in which we abbreviate
(\Gamma\mathrm{Fin}_{\mathrm{free}})_{/X}:=\Gamma\mathrm{Fin}_{\mathrm{free}}\times_{\Gamma\mathcal{S}}\Gamma\mathcal{S}_{/X}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{\Gamma}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Gamma\mathrm{Fin}_{\mathrm{free}})_{/X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{free}}_{\emptyset}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma\mathrm{Fin}_{\mathrm{free}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\mathfrak{A}}^{\Gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{d}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{A}^{\Gamma}}

We further denote by
\Re^{\Gamma}_{X}\colon{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{\Gamma}(X)\to(\Gamma\mathcal{S}_{X/})^{\simeq}

the functor sending
(G,A\to X)
to the pushout of
\Gamma
-spaces
\Re^{\Gamma}(G)\sqcup_{A}X
, where the
\Gamma
-equivariant map
A\to\Re(G)
is
\beta^{\Gamma}\alpha^{\Gamma}(G)
. When
\Gamma=1
we omit it from the notation.

Intuitively, an object in
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{\Gamma}(X)
is the datum of a graph
G
with a free
\Gamma
-action admitting finitely many free orbits of edges and vertices, a
\Gamma
-subset
A
of its vertices, and a map of
\Gamma
-spaces
A\to X
; a morphism is given by collapsing trees and changing the attaching subset of vertices in the
\infty
-category
(\Gamma\mathrm{Fin}_{\mathrm{free}})_{/X}
.
The functor
\Re^{\Gamma}_{X}
constructs a
\Gamma
-space by attaching the realisation of
G
to
X
along
A
. The fact that morphisms in
\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma}_{\emptyset}
are essentially given by collapsing trees implies that
\Re^{\Gamma}_{X}
takes values in the core groupoid of
\Gamma\mathcal{S}_{X/}
.

Remark 7.22
.

We may give an alternative to Definition
7.21
in the spirit of Remark
7.18
. The
\Gamma
-space
X\in\Gamma\mathcal{S}
corresponds to the object
X/\Gamma\in\mathcal{S}_{/\mathbf{B}\Gamma}
; we may then identify
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{\Gamma}(X)
with the fibre product
\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{free}}_{\emptyset}\times_{\mathrm{Fin}_{/\mathbf{B}\Gamma}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
, where the first leg is the functor
\mathfrak{A}^{\Gamma}\colon\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{free}}_{\emptyset}\to\Gamma\mathrm{Fin}_{\mathrm{free}}
.

Lemma 7.23
.

For
X\in\mathcal{S}
we have equivalences of spaces

\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(\emptyset,X)\simeq|{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)|
.

Proof.

By Remark
7.20
we have that
\tilde{\mathfrak{A}}\colon{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)\to\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
is the pullback of a cocartesian fibration, so it is again a cocartesian fibration; moreover, the straightening of
\tilde{\mathfrak{A}}
is the composite
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(\emptyset,-)\circ d_{0}\colon\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
. We now recall from
[
9
, Theorem 8.4]
that the composite functor
\mathrm{Fin}\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(\emptyset,-)}\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}\xrightarrow{|-|}\mathcal{S}
can be identified with
\mathrm{Gr}(\emptyset,-)\colon\mathrm{Fin}\to\mathcal{S}
. It follows that the localisation of
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)
at the “vertical” wide subcategory
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)\times_{\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}}(\mathrm{Fin}_{/X})^{\simeq}
is the left fibration over
\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
corresponding to the composite functor
\mathrm{Gr}(\emptyset,-)\circ d_{0}\colon\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}\to\mathcal{S}
; the total
\infty
-category of this left fibration is
\mathcal{C}_{y/}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{Fin}_{/X}
, where we abbreviate
\mathcal{C}=\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{S}}
and
y=(\emptyset\to\emptyset)
, and we also rely on Corollary
7.12
; by applying Proposition
7.4
we obtain the statement.
∎

In the light of the equivalence from Lemma
7.23
, the map of spaces
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(\emptyset,X)\to(\mathcal{S}_{X/})^{\simeq}
induced by
D_{0}
agrees with the map induced on classifying spaces by the functor
\Re_{X}\colon{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)\to(\mathcal{S}_{X/})^{\simeq}
.

Our last goal is to prove the following proposition.

Proposition 7.24
.

Let
Y
and
X
be spaces with
X
aspherical. Then the functor
D_{0}\colon\mathrm{GrCob}\to\mathrm{Cospan}(\mathcal{S})
induces at the level of morphism spaces an inclusion of spaces
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(Y,X)\hookrightarrow(\mathcal{S}_{Y\sqcup X/})^{\simeq}
, with essential image spanned by those cospans of spaces
Y\to W\leftarrow X
such that
W
may be obtained from
X
by attaching finitely many 0-cells and 1-cells.

Proof.

By Remark
7.13
it suffices to consider the case
Y=\emptyset
. It is evident that a cospan of spaces
Y\to W\leftarrow X
is in the essential image if and only if the pair
(W,X)
satisfies the given property. We may therefore fix
\bar{W}\in(\mathcal{S}_{X/})^{\simeq}
and aim at proving that the fibre at
\bar{W}
of the map
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(\emptyset,X)\to(\mathcal{S}_{X/})^{\simeq}
is contractible.

If
\bar{W}
is disconnected, write
\bar{W}=\coprod_{i\in I}\bar{W}_{i}
, and decompose
X=\coprod_{i\in I}\bar{X}_{i}
by letting
\bar{X}_{i}
be the preimage of
\bar{W}_{i}
in
X
. Then we have an identification

\mathrm{fib}_{\bar{W}}(\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(\emptyset,X)\to(\mathcal{S}_{X/})^{\simeq})\simeq\prod_{i\in I}\mathrm{fib}_{\bar{W}_{i}}(\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(\emptyset,\bar{X}_{i})\to(\mathcal{S}_{\bar{X}_{i}/})^{\simeq}),

where all but finitely many factors in the product are contractible (so the above is essentially a finite product): this follows from the hypothesis that
\bar{W}
can be obtained from
X
by attaching
finitely many
cells, and the fact that if
\bar{X}_{i}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\bar{W}_{i}
, then Corollary
7.11
implies that
\mathrm{fib}_{\bar{W}_{i}}(\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(\emptyset,\bar{X}_{i})\to(\mathcal{S}_{\bar{X}_{i}/})^{\simeq})
is contractible.

Thus it suffices to prove the claim under the additional hypothesis that
\bar{W}
is connected. This also implies that
\pi_{0}(X)
is finite, because it must be possible to make
X
connected by attaching finitely many cells. Let therefore
X=\coprod_{i=1}^{p}X_{i}
, with
X_{i}\simeq B\Gamma_{i}
for
p\geq 0
and for some discrete groups
\Gamma_{i}
. We stress that some of the
\Gamma_{i}
may be the trivial group; if however this happens for all
0\leq i\leq p
, then the equivalence
\mathrm{fib}_{\bar{W}}(\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(\emptyset,X)\to(\mathcal{S}_{X/})^{\simeq})
follows already from Corollary
7.12
. We may therefore assume that
p\geq 1
and, without loss of generality, that
\Gamma_{1}
is not the trivial group. Similarly, if
\chi(\bar{W},X)=0
, then
X\xrightarrow{\simeq}\bar{W}
and the equivalence

\mathrm{fib}_{\bar{W}}(\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(\emptyset,X)\to(\mathcal{S}_{X/})^{\simeq})
follows already from Corollary
7.11
. We may therefore assume
\chi(\bar{W},X)<0
: concretely this means that if
\bar{W}\simeq\bigvee_{i=1}^{p}X_{i}\vee\bigvee_{j=1}^{k}S^{1}
, then
p+k\geq 2
. We now denote by
\Gamma
the free product

\Gamma:=\bigast_{i=1}^{p}\Gamma_{i}*\bigast_{j=1}^{k}\mathbb{Z}

and fix an identification
(X\to\bar{W})\simeq(\coprod_{i=1}^{p}\mathbf{B}\Gamma_{i}\to\mathbf{B}\Gamma)
in
\mathrm{Fun}([1],\mathcal{S})
. We let
\tilde{X}:=X\times_{\mathbf{B}\Gamma}*
; the equivalence
\Gamma\mathcal{S}\simeq\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{B}\Gamma}
makes
\tilde{X}
into an object in
\Gamma\mathcal{S}
, and we observe that
\tilde{X}
in fact belongs to
\Gamma\mathrm{Fin}\subset\Gamma\mathcal{S}
; more precisely, the above data give a canonical identification
\tilde{X}\simeq\coprod_{i=1}^{p}\Gamma/\Gamma_{i}
.

By Lemma
7.23
we may compute
\mathrm{fib}_{\bar{W}}(\mathfrak{M}_{\mathrm{Gr}}(\emptyset,X)\to(\mathcal{S}_{X/})^{\simeq})
as the classifying space of the
\infty
-category
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)_{\mathbf{B}\Gamma}
defined by the left pullback square in the following diagram in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
, or equivalently, as the total pullback:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)_{\mathbf{B}\Gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{S}_{X/})^{\simeq}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{X/}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}\Gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Re_{X}}

We may regard
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)_{\mathbf{B}\Gamma}
as a full
\infty
-subcategory of the lax pullback
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)^{\prime}_{\mathbf{B}\Gamma}
of the same diagram above, i.e. the following pullback on left, using the under-overcategory
\mathcal{S}_{X//\mathbf{B}\Gamma}
defined by the pullback on right:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)^{\prime}_{\mathbf{B}\Gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{X//\mathbf{B}\Gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{X/};}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Re_{X}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{X//\mathbf{B}\Gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}([2],\mathcal{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Fun}([1],\mathcal{S}).}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X\to\mathbf{B}\Gamma)}

We may now identify
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)^{\prime}_{\mathbf{B}\Gamma}
with
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{\Gamma}(\tilde{X})
. Concretely, we have a functor
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{\Gamma}(\tilde{X})\to{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)^{\prime}_{\mathbf{B}\Gamma}
sending a pair
(G,A\to\tilde{X})
, consisting of a
\Gamma
-gaf together with a
\Gamma
-equivariant map
A\to\tilde{X}
, to the quotient pair
(G/\Gamma,A/\Gamma\to\tilde{X}/\Gamma\simeq X)
, where
G/\Gamma:=(A/\Gamma,V/\Gamma,H/\Gamma,\sigma/\Gamma,\upsilon/\Gamma)
; the pushout
\Re(G/\Gamma)\sqcup_{A/\Gamma}X
is endowed with a canonical map to
\mathbf{B}\Gamma
. The inverse functor
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)^{\prime}_{\mathbf{B}\Gamma}\to{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{\Gamma}(\tilde{X})
sends a triple
(G,A\to X,\Re(G)\sqcup_{A}X\to\mathbf{B}\Gamma)
, consisting of a gaf
G
, a map
A\to X
, and a map
\Re_{X}(G)\sqcup_{A}X\to\mathbf{B}\Gamma
, to the pair
(\tilde{G},\tilde{A}\to\tilde{X})
, where
\tilde{A}=A\times_{\mathbf{B}\Gamma}*
,
\tilde{V}=V\times_{\mathbf{B}\Gamma}*
etc., and where the map
\tilde{A}\to\tilde{X}
is the image of
A\to X
along the pullback functor
-\times_{\mathbf{B}\Gamma}*
.

In particular
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)^{\prime}_{\mathbf{B}\Gamma}
, and hence
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)_{\mathbf{B}\Gamma}
, are plain categories. Along the equivalence
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)^{\prime}\simeq{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{\Gamma}(\tilde{X})
, the full subcategory
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)_{\mathbf{B}\Gamma}\subset{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}(X)^{\prime}_{\mathbf{B}\Gamma}
corresponds to the full subcategory
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{tree}}(\tilde{X})
spanned by pairs
(G,A\to\tilde{X})
whose image along
\Re_{\tilde{X}}^{\Gamma}
is a
\Gamma
-space under
X
whose underlying space is contractible; we think of
\Re(G)\sqcup_{A}\tilde{X}
as a tree (i.e. a contractible graph) endowed with a
\Gamma
-action that satisfies the following:

•

there are finitely many
\Gamma
-orbits of vertices and edges,

•

there are
p
marked and pairwise distinct orbits of vertices, each endowed with an identification with the finite
\Gamma
-set
\Gamma/\Gamma_{i}
;

•

all other orbits of vertices, as well as all orbits of edges, are free.

Our goal is to show that
|{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{tree}}(\tilde{X})|\simeq*
. Note that we have a restricted cocartesian fibration
\tilde{\mathfrak{A}}^{\Gamma}\colon{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{tree}}(\tilde{X})\to\Gamma\mathrm{Fin}_{\mathrm{free}}
.
We may consider the full subcategory of

{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{tree}}(\tilde{X})_{\mathrm{red}}\subset{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{tree}}(\tilde{X})
spanned by objects
(G,A\to\tilde{X})
satisfying the following property: for all
a\in A
, the preimage
\sigma^{-1}(a)\cap H
is a singleton. The inclusion
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{tree}}(\tilde{X})_{\mathrm{red}}\subset{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{tree}}(\tilde{X})
admits a right adjoint, sending a generic pair
(G,A\to\tilde{X})
to the pair
(G^{\prime},A^{\prime}\to\tilde{X})
where
G^{\prime}:=(A^{\prime},V,H,\sigma^{\prime},\upsilon^{\prime})
,
A^{\prime}:=\sigma^{-1}(A)\cap H
,
\sigma^{\prime}
agrees with the identity on
A^{\prime}\subset H
and with
\sigma
elsewhere, and
\upsilon^{\prime}
agrees with the identity on
A^{\prime}
and with
\upsilon
elsewhere; the counit of the adjunction
(G^{\prime},A^{\prime}\to\tilde{X})\to(G,A\to\tilde{X})
is the
\tilde{\mathfrak{A}}^{\Gamma}
-cocartesian lift of
\sigma\colon A^{\prime}\to A
at
(G^{\prime},A^{\prime}\to\tilde{X})
. Letting
\mathrm{Gaf}_{\emptyset,\tilde{X}}^{\Gamma}
denote the fibre at
\tilde{X}\in\Gamma\mathrm{Fin}
of the functor
\mathfrak{A}^{\Gamma}\colon\mathrm{Gaf}_{\emptyset}^{\Gamma}\to\Gamma\mathrm{Fin}
, we may now identify
{\mathbb{G}\mathrm{r}}^{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{tree}}(\tilde{X})_{\mathrm{red}}
with the full subcategory
\mathrm{Gaf}_{\emptyset,\tilde{X}}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{tree}}\subset\mathrm{Gaf}_{\emptyset,\tilde{X}}^{\Gamma}
spanned by objects
G
such that the underlying space of the
\Gamma
-space
E\sqcup_{H}(\tilde{X}\sqcup V)
is contractible.

We may consider the full subcategory
\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\min}_{\emptyset,\tilde{X}}\subset\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{tree}}_{\emptyset,\tilde{X}}
spanned by objects
G
such that
G
admits no
leaves
; here by a
leaf
we mean an element
v
in the set of
V
of inner vertices of
G
whose valence is
1
, i.e. such that
\sigma^{-1}(v)\cap H
is a singleton. If we think of a generic object of
\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{tree}}_{\emptyset,\tilde{X}}
as a tree with
\Gamma
-action endowed with a
\Gamma
-equivariant inclusion of
\tilde{X}
into the
\Gamma
-set of vertices, then for an object in
\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\min}_{\emptyset,\tilde{X}}
we are requiring that the
\Gamma
-action on the tree be
minimal
, in the sense that there is no proper
\Gamma
-equivariant subtree containing
\tilde{X}
.
The inclusion
\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\min}_{\emptyset,\tilde{X}}\subset\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{tree}}_{\emptyset,\tilde{X}}
admits a left adjoint, sending
G
to the
\Gamma
-gaf
G^{\prime}
obtained from
G
by repeatedly collapsing leaves along with their associated edges.
Our goal becomes therefore to show that
|\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\min}_{\emptyset,\tilde{X}}|\simeq*
.

We finally let
\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\min}_{\emptyset,\tilde{X},\geq 3}\subset\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\min}_{\emptyset,\tilde{X}}
denote the full subcategory spanned by objects
G
such that for all
v\in V
, the valence of
v
, i.e. the cardinality of
\sigma^{-1}(v)\cap H
, is at least 3. We have a functor
F\colon\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\min}_{\emptyset,\tilde{X}}\to\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\min}_{\emptyset,\tilde{X},\geq 3}
, sending
G
to the
\Gamma
-gaf
G^{\prime}
obtained from
G
by removing all inner vertices of valence 2 and concatenating the edges accordingly. We have that
F
is a cocartesian fibration; the fibre
F^{-1}(G^{\prime})
can be thought of as the category of subdivisions of edges of
G^{\prime}
, and is equivalent to
\mathbf{\Delta}^{E^{\prime}}
, where
\mathbf{\Delta}
denotes the simplex category; in particular all fibres of
F
have contractible classifying space, so that we have
|\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\min}_{\emptyset,\tilde{X}}|\simeq|\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\min}_{\emptyset,\tilde{X},\leq 3}|
.

We conclude by arguing that
|\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\min}_{\emptyset,\tilde{X},\leq 3}|\simeq*
. In
[
38
, Section 4]
, Guirardel–Levitt introduce a topological space
P\mathcal{O}
associated with a free product of groups
\Gamma
as above, under the condition that
p\geq 1
,
p+k\geq 2
, and that
all factors
\Gamma_{i}
are non-trivial groups
.

Points in
P\mathcal{O}
are given by isometry classes of metric simplicial
\Gamma
-trees
T
such that the action of
\Gamma
on
T
has finitely many orbits of vertices and edges and it is minimal (i.e.
T
has no leaves). As trees are considered as a particular type of metric spaces, rather than as combinatorial objects, vertices of valence 2 are not allowed in
T
, or otherwise said, they are considered as points inside a longer edge, unless they have a non-trivial stabiliser. For the same reason, a tree
T
is identified with any tree
T^{\prime}
obtained by actually collapsing edges of length 0, as the underlying metric spaces are
\Gamma
-equivariantly, and uniquely, isometric.
The “
P
” in the notation “
P\mathcal{O}
” refers to the fact that the length function is considered as a projective function, i.e. up to rescaling by positive real numbers.
A metric simplicial
\Gamma
-tree
T
must satisfy the following additional property in order to represent a point in
P\mathcal{O}
: for all
1\leq i\leq p

there is
exactly one vertex
v_{i}
in
T
whose stabiliser is
\Gamma_{i}
, whereas all other points of
T
have trivial stabilisers; clearly,
v_{i}\neq v_{j}
if
i\neq j
because
\Gamma_{i}\neq\Gamma_{j}
as subgroups of
\Gamma
, using that all
\Gamma_{i}
are non-trivial.

We have emphasized two conditions that differ in the setting of Guirardel–Levitt from our setting: all
\Gamma_{i}
are assumed to be non-trivial groups, and for
T\in P\mathcal{O}
the point
v_{i}\in T
is not considered as an additional datum, but a property of the tree
T
, namely the unique fixpoint of
\Gamma_{i}
. We now observe that the construction of Guirardel–Levitt makes sense also if we instead relax the condition that all
\Gamma_{i}\neq 1
, and if we specify as data of
T
the vertices
v_{i}
that have
\Gamma_{i}
as stabiliser: for all
\Gamma_{i}\neq 1
there will be a unique choice if there is a choice at all (as we still require stabilisers of edges to be trivial), but for all
\Gamma_{i}=1
we are choosing a point
v_{i}
in
T
with free
\Gamma
-orbit; we require that the points
v_{1},\dots,v_{p}
be distinct.

The proof that
P\mathcal{O}
is contractible extends to this setting with minor modifications. If we now take the “barycentric spine” of
P\mathcal{O}
, we obtain a topological space homeomorphic to the geometric realisation of the nerve of a skeleton of the category
\mathrm{Gaf}^{\Gamma,\min}_{\emptyset,\tilde{X},\leq 3}
, in which we select one object in each isomorphism class. This concludes the proof.
∎
